# Proof map: the load-bearing skeleton of the Lean archive

Working note for the book. It records, top down from `src/Main.lean`, the chapter-sized blocks
that carry the argument, where each block lives in the archive, what it does mathematically,
how much of it *we* have read, and what (if anything) plays the same role in Masullo's draft.

- **Archive.** `lean.tar.gz` joined from the 14 parts at `https://alpo.ge/lean.tar.gz.partNN`,
  239,275,554 bytes, sha256 `9631b4f020199e580ba86178cb56c6e9b09f690c2369a80dc4334aa93f49c7fc`
  (re-downloaded and re-verified 2026-11-21 04:01–04:06 UTC; see `sources/SOURCES.md`).
  Paths below are relative to the archive's `lean/` directory, so `src/Main.lean:4` means line 4
  of `src/Main.lean` inside `lean/`. Toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.33.1`; Mathlib pinned at
  `0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474` (tag v4.33.1).
- **Masullo.** *The Music of the Primes*, 940 pp., sha256 `e039d3f6…97bf5`; section numbers and
  printed page numbers (they coincide with PDF page numbers).
- **How the map was made.** A text-level index of all 1,057,807 declaration headers and the
  import graph of all 175,187 files (scripts and outputs in the run directory, see the end of
  this note), then a manual walk through proof terms starting at `src/Main.lean`. Community guides,
  in particular the LUMI docs (`aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi`, `docs/02-proof-map.md`), were
  used for navigation only; every file:line below was located in our own extraction, and where
  our line numbers differ from a community guide we give ours.

## Reading levels used in this note

| Level | Meaning | Book tag it supports |
|---|---|---|
| **R3** | we read the declaration and its whole proof term | `[Lean, read]` |
| **R2** | we read the statement (type) and the top of the proof | `[Lean]` for the statement only |
| **R1** | we located the declaration and its type at file:line; the description of its *content* comes from the announcement or a community guide | `[Lean]` for existence; content is `[Paper]` (announcement), `[Community]` (community guide) or `[Open]` |

"Import cone" = number of archive modules transitively imported by the file that contains the
node's main declaration (text-level import graph; 175,187 modules in total). It is an upper
bound on what the node *can* use, not a measured dependency. Measured per-declaration
dependency counts exist in the LUMI audit (`results/per-declaration-dependencies.tsv`); we
have not reproduced them.

## The skeleton on one screen

```
P0  five target theorems (src/Main.lean:4,6,8,10,12) ── statements in src/Statements.lean:3,6,10,14 + Mathlib's RiemannHypothesis
 ▲
P1  functional-equation reductions (short, Mathlib only)
 ▲
P2  assembly: EveryCharacter  :=  L(s,χ) ≠ 0 for Re s > 1/2, all N, all χ
 ▲
P3  primitive → all characters (Euler factors)
 ▲
P4  zero-free engine: per cyclic "level", let ε → 0
 ▲            ▲
P5  Landau-window criterion         P6  core_gm:  Gm(x) ≤ x^{1/2+(eD+eL+7)ε} on windows
    (one-sided prime-sum bound           ▲  hypotheses: host data (EAtH) + chain data (CoreAtH)
     on windows ⇒ zero-free)             │
                                         ├── P13 auxiliary prime pairs (zero density, mean squares, Siegel)
                                         └── P7  chain data: m ≈ √x lifts, SizeData₂ clauses
                                               ├── P8  divisibility at inert primes (Conclusion1)
                                               ├── P9  slope inequality / heights (Bost)
                                               ├── P10 near-orthogonality of periods (LinkS, LinkG)
                                               └── P11 congruence modules ≤ Selmer + Ш (LinkN, HypV)
                                                      ▲
                                                     P12 window bound VWin on Ш of CM fields
                                                      ▲
P17 theta-lift chain families (Hyps)   P14 Galois representations (Row0b)   P15 partial local Langlands
P16 Green's classification (finite GL_d)   P18 "literature layer": ~250 named theorems proved as Props
```

The two halves have very different sizes. The analytic endgame (P3–P6, P13) sits in import
cones of 10 to about 1,300 modules; the arithmetic engine behind the hypotheses of `core_gm`
(P7–P12, P14–P17) sits in cones of up to 157,603 modules. Everything hard is hidden in Props
that `core_gm` takes as hypotheses and that are *proved* elsewhere in the archive.

## Notes on individual nodes

**P16 and choice of coordinates.** The map in §13.4.9 takes (I1) through
`exists_cusp_of_regular`, then compares the root sums on every invariant
restriction in the common finite layer. The recursive transport is an
expository proof, with the final GL comparison using (I4) and (I5). The
fixed-reference-root comparison `exists_beta` acts by the square of the
multiplicative automorphism. `typeChar_full_of_torus` takes
`CuspidalCharacterFormula` as an explicit hypothesis. Section 13.4.12 adds the
two parallel naming and label supplies: `UpperTheory.Zero.LLC.nameLaws` (proving
orbit uniqueness via Parseval-plus-shift `pin` and ring-isomorphism transport
for Chapter 12's `parEx`/`parUniq`) and `PM.greenCuspidal` (proving all-primes
Newton power-sum label uniqueness and separating what existence + degree,
injectivity, and exhaustion supply to `GreenSeries.green_series`,
`cuspParam_of_green`, and `eSer_eq`). The reading scope and deeper inputs are
listed in the Chapter 13 source tables; §13.4.15 discharges **(U4)** (`zIdemOrthogonal_holds` / `central_idempotent_properties`) and returns `iso_of_label` to `cuspParam_all`, `PM.greenCuspidal`, and `PendTie.greenO`, leaving **(U3)** as the sole remaining R2 interface on `PM.greenCuspidal` itself.

**P16 and the Gram count.** [§13.4.6.1](../chapters/13-greens-theorem-and-the-literature-layer.md#13461-counting-invariant-subspaces-and-cyclic-vectors-for-the-gram-matrix) reads the nine suppliers formerly listed as [Open] in §13.4.6. Local R3 covers the three translation sums at calls 111, 118 and 128 in `IsPrimitiveEndClassFunctionInvariantSubspaceMobi.lean`, cyclic mass and vector partition at calls 83 and 92 in `CharpolyEndomorphismDensityGLRecursion.lean`, block normalization at calls 102 and 153 in `EndomorphismMassMulParabolicInductionTrivial.lean`, and the primary profile and length matching in `ConjOfPrimaryFinrankKerPowEq.lean:26–34`. The exact boundary and the deeper [Lean]/[Open] interfaces are in the section table. The finite certificate checks the two sample dimensions.

**P16 and the positive sign.** Steps 3–4 of Proposition 13.6 (§13.4.10) use three facts on
$\mathrm{GL}_d(F)$: an integral class function of norm one is $\pm$ an irreducible character (I8),
the identity value $\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}(1-q^i)$ obtained by counting all subspaces (I2), and
$\overline{\chi(x)}=\chi(x^{-1})$, which turns the Gram norm into Mathlib's `FDRep` simplicity
criterion. Only the third part of `extZero_spec` (units of the endomorphism ring versus the matrix
group) is used on that positive-sign path, whereas all four parts of `extZero_spec` (including
vanishing on singular stabilizer endomorphisms and conjugation invariance) are consumed in §13.4.13's
direct proof of `cuspElliptic_statement` (which establishes elliptic character determination via
integer Gram vectors and Parseval/Vandermonde/modulo-63 separation without invoking `exists_regular`).
The converse `exists_regular` is read in §13.4.14: it uses parts (1), (3) and (4) of `extZero_spec`, the integer Gram
vector of §13.4.13 and the integrality of pairings with the elementary-symmetric virtual characters to put a nonzero
Gram coefficient on a regular residue, and it closes with Schur orthogonality against the realized character.

**P0–P1 (read in full).** The four definitions of `src/Statements.lean` and Mathlib's
`RiemannHypothesis` are the whole trusted statement surface. `src/Main.lean:6` proves
`RiemannZetaZerosOnLine` with the same term `rhOfAll` that proves Mathlib's `RiemannHypothesis`
at `src/Main.lean:4`, so the kernel has checked that the two Props are definitionally equal; the
same holds for `DirichletLNonvanishingRightOfHalf` versus `UpperTheory.EveryCharacter`
(`src/Main.lean:8`). The reductions in `ClaimStrip.lean` import only `HalfPlane.lean`, which imports
only Mathlib. Detail and prose: Chapter 1.

limit, the cosine recurrence and normalized sine-product limit wrapper, the Gamma reflection
formula, and the real/nonreal split proving `Complex.Gamma_ne_zero`. The positive-real-part
specialization feeds `Complex.Gammaℝ_ne_zero_of_re_pos` and the parity reader. The normalized-power theorem used by this cosine specialization is read in [§15.4.3.10](../chapters/15-two-proofs-side-by-side.md#154310-how-powers-of-a-unique-peak-concentrate-at-a-point). **P2 (two assembly routes).** The archive contains a second assembly of `EveryCharacter`,
`UpperTheory.Top.plugAll` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/EveryCharacter.lean:16`),
and a second derivation of RH, `UpperTheory.FinalTheorems.riemannHypothesis`
(`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/MainConclusion.lean:13`) via
`UpperTheory.riemannHypothesis_of_everyCharacter`
(`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/RiemannHypothesisOfEveryCharacter.lean:215`).
`src/Main.lean` uses neither: its route is `src/Supplements/Kernels/MainTheorems.lean:7` →
`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/MainConclusion.lean:10` → `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/EveryCharacterOfLiteratureOfVWin.lean:810` →
`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/SiegelEisensteinSecondTermHypotheses.lean:69` → `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopPlug.lean:16`. (The LUMI compiled-term walk
reports 146,437 of 175,187 project modules used in the closure of `src/Main.lean` [Community]; which
specific side routes lie outside that closure we have not checked ourselves [Open].)

**P4–P6 (the logical shape of the endgame).** In `zeroFreeAll_of_topG` the three inputs of
`zeroFreeOn_of_tiedWin_cyc` are: `level_gm_le_on_family_scales` (P6), `formB` (P5), and the
window-scale data obtained from `TopG` and the host supply `supplyChi` (P13, P12). `TopG` is
not an analytic statement; it is proved from Green's classification and the theta-lift
hypotheses (P16, P17) in `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopPlug.lean:12`. This is the point where the arithmetic engine
enters the analytic endgame.

**P6 (primitive values, §5.3.1.1).** `prim_read` consumes `primitive_value_primitive`
at `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/PrimitiveCosets/PrimRead.lean:56–61`.
The coefficient theorem at
`src/Formalism/SizeWindowBrackets/ChainSlopeWindows/GaloisMapComplexConjugation.lean:233`
uses `cosetMean_eq_primitive` (:209), with the conductor and subgroup orthogonality
steps at :129,145,155,167. These bodies were read for the
[Chapter 5 teaching section](../chapters/05-the-square-root-barrier-core-gm.md#5311-why-inertia-averaging-recovers-the-primitive-character).
Host-field inertia and Frobenius congruence inputs retain [Lean].

**P6 → P7 (where the weight is).** `core_gm` has a small import cone (114 modules) because its
content is in its hypotheses: `EAtH` and `CoreAtH` are Props. Their proofs come from the
families built in P17 and the clauses of P7–P11; they are attached in
`tiedCore_of_chainTied` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/PrimitiveCosets/LevelGmLeOnFamilyScales.lean:13`). A book chapter on `core_gm` can be
honest and short; the chapters behind its hypotheses cannot.

**P7 (Clause 7 on the main path, §6.4.5).** In the main-path record `Mod.data`
(`src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/QuadraticLogDerivCongr.lean:464–504`) the weights `d` are
the ranks of the lattices `div.Nu u`, and `muN`/`muM` are `HLat.mu` of `div.Nu u` and `(fm u).lat 0`.
Clause 7 is `model_slopes_five` (:677–685), a pure transport of `SlopesN_at` (:181–183) to the base
index; `linkCore` builds it as `L7` (`src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/LossesSmallShares.lean:198–200`).
`s_eq` (:687–691) identifies `Data.s` with `sFrame`
(`src/Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataArith.lean:131–132`). The linear step closing `s_lower5` has the
certificate $h_1+h_2+h_4+h_5+h_6$, all multipliers 1, and none of the five can be dropped. In the
main-theorem export (sha256 `d5fad832…89fb`) `PointwiseBoundVariant` is reachable from
`riemannHypothesis`. `Data.MeanBoundSecondForm`, `Data.SlopesN`, `MeanBoundWith`,
`MeanBoundOfLossBounds`, `weighted_mean_bound_statement` and `uniform_bound_statement` are absent.
The same cone facts appear in `leanprover-community/riemann-hypothesis` issue #5. The
producers `HypLow`/`HypHigh` behind `Places_at`
(`src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/TopDensityNGeSixteen.lean:1516–1525`) stay [Lean].

**P8 (line-number discrepancy).** The LUMI map cites `src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GrowthBounds/WeilJacquetShalikaLaw.lean:101` for
`Conclusion1`; in our extraction the `def Conclusion1` header is at line 97 (its last line is
101). Likewise `LinkS` is at `src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalTheoremInputs.lean:152` in our extraction (LUMI: 145).
We cite ours.

**P12 (independence supply).** Chapter 11 §11.4.3.1 reads the bodies of `sinnott`, `nonvan`, and `memZ` (R3). Finite differences and the sum over sign representatives yield the reduced twist. Uniform nonvanishing supplies the ideal relation used by `cyc`. See the section’s pinned source map for the named inputs that retain [Lean].

**P12 (class-group rank supply).** Chapter 11 §11.4.3.2 reads `cyc` and its direct producers (R3). Multiplying the `memZ` membership by $1-[J]$ and using Stickelberger annihilation (`stickH`) shows that $(1-\gamma)^N$ maps $(1-J)\mathrm{Cl}(L)$ into its $\ell$-multiples (`core`). A nilpotent length estimate (`nilCard`, `card_range_inf_torsion_le_index_pow`) bounds $\lvert(1-J)\mathrm{Cl}(L)\cap\mathrm{Cl}(L)[\ell]\rvert$ by the $N$-th power of the ambiguous quotient $[\mathrm{Cl}(L):\ell\mathrm{Cl}(L)+(1-\gamma)\mathrm{Cl}(L)]$, which `ambCyc` bounds by the number of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-characters of $G_F$ unramified outside $\ell$, $F=\mathbb Q(\zeta_{f\ell^e})$. For odd $\ell$, `reflOdd` and `pOdd` give $[\mathrm{Cl}:\mathrm{Cl}^\ell]\le\ell B^{2N}$. For $\ell=2$, `sgnCyc` bounds $[\mathrm{Cl}^-:(1-J)\mathrm{Cl}]$ by $2^{\varphi(4f)N}$ and `pTwo` gives $(2^{\varphi(4f)N}B^N)^2$. Stickelberger's theorem, the Artin-map and Kummer inputs, and the unit-sign inputs keep [Lean].

**P12 (size).** `VwinOfRow.lean` (6 lines) imports a cone of 157,603 modules, 90% of the
archive. By import weight the window bound is the largest single component of the proof.

**P18 (fidelity of names).** That a Prop is called `PoitouTateDuality` does not make it Poitou–Tate
duality. The LUMI doc 07 reports verdicts on 105 such statements, and Chapter 18 provides 14
full `R3` statement-fidelity case studies (`GlobalArtinReciprocity`, `HeckeLAdicCharacter`,
`RayClassFieldSt`/`RayClassFieldMaximalSt`, `ChebotarevDegreeOne`, `FerreroWashington`,
`SiegelKlingenDeligneRibet`, `Kato`, `PoitouTateNormalised`/`PoitouTateDuality`, `ClozelPurity`,
`BaseChangeDefiniteUnitary`, `WeilSiegelFormula`/`SiegelWeilUnitaryConvergent`, and
`DeRhamPotSemistable`). Every remaining unreviewed "literature" input stays tagged `[Lean]` for
the Prop that is proved and `[Open]` for the claim that it matches the published theorem until
compared line by line.

## Masullo's route in the same format (for the comparison chapters)

| Masullo | Content (our summary) | Role relative to the Lean skeleton |
|---|---|---|
| Chs. 2–3 | adelic θ-functions and idelic zeta functions (Tate's thesis, functional equation, Weil's identity) | P1 (functional equation): same classical content |
| Chs. 4–9 | Schauder bases, spaces of θ-functions, local–global structure, orbits, Hardy–Sobolev topologies | no Lean counterpart |
| Ch. 10 | trace formula, spectral side (zeros counted with multiplicity on a Hilbert-space completion) | no Lean counterpart; known gap at p. 451 after (10.3.1.18) (resting on Terminology 10.2.1.2(2), p. 444): Yan Ru Pei (`doi:10.5281/zenodo.24409811`) proposes an unreviewed partial repair on the critical line $I = \{0\}$ only [Community], leaving the positive-width strip case $I = [-\alpha, \alpha]$ needed by Theorem 12.5.1.1 unrepaired [Open] |
| Chs. 11–12 | shell structure; trace formula, geometric side; Main Theorem 12.5.1.1 (p. 519) | no Lean counterpart |

| Ch. 14 | multiplicities of zeros, function fields, higher rank | none; §14.2 (function fields) is relevant to the Stepanov motivation of P7–P8 |
| App. B | convexity bound, explicit formulas, zero-free regions | P5 and P13 (classical analytic inputs) |
| App. B, Prop. B.4.1.2 (pp. 608–610) | Logarithmic periodization and midpoint samples at jumps (§14.4.2) | Classical Mathlib correspondence [Lean, read] for `Real.fourierCoeff_tsum_comp_add` and `Real.tsum_eq_tsum_fourier` at the fixed pin. Jump convergence is a literature input. The application to Ch. 11 orbital traces is [Open]. |
| §1.2.3 (1), p. 16; §12.4.1, (12.4.1.16)–(12.4.1.23), p. 518 | the diagonal weight $\lvert x\rvert_{F,S}^{2a}$ against the multiplicative Tamagawa measure $\mu_S=c_S\lvert\cdot\rvert_{F,S}^{-1}\nu_S$; $a=1/2$ returns the additive measure (§15.4.3.1); $c_S$ is a ratio of two Haar measures (§15.4.3.3) | Classical Mathlib correspondence [Lean, read] for `distribHaarChar`, `haarScalarFactor`, `isMulLeftInvariant_eq_smul_of_regular`, `haarMeasure_unique`, `Real.map_volume_mul_left`, `addHaar_smul`, `integral_comp_mul_left_Ioi`, `mellin_comp_mul_left`, `IsHaarMeasure.isInvInvariant_of_regular`, `IsHaarMeasure.nullSingletonClass` at the fixed pin. Local only: no bearing on the $\alpha$-independence of Theorem 12.5.1.1 [Open]. |

The two arguments share only the classical frame (functional equations, explicit-formula-type
links between zeros and primes). Their engines are disjoint: an adelic trace formula on
$C_F$ versus a Stepanov-type auxiliary construction made of theta lifts, controlled by
Iwasawa theory and Galois cohomology.

## Reproducibility

Run directory: `/Users/lihongmin/Hermes/state/quota/runs/rhbook-architecture-11211200/`.

- `fetch_lean_parts.sh`, `fetch_lean_parts.log`, `headers/` — download, per-part sha256 and ETag.
- `build_index.py` → `index/decls.tsv.gz` (name, kind, file, line), `index/imports.tsv.gz`,
  `index/files.tsv.gz`; 24 s CPU.
- `index/import_cones.json` — the import-cone sizes quoted above.
- `static_scan.py` → `static_scan.json` — text scan used in Chapter 1; 123 s CPU.
- `check_citations.py` → `citations_report.txt` — machine check that every `src/…:line` and
  `Mathlib/…:line` in `chapters/` and `notes/` resolves to an existing line of the pinned
  sources and that a declaration named nearby appears on that line; the citations it cannot
  match automatically (a cited `import` line, a docstring, a name only in a code block) are
  listed for manual review and were checked by hand before each commit.
- `kexactB_family_files.txt` — the 72 files that mention `kexactB`, `kc%`, `tr_via` or
  `tr_clause` (Chapter 1, Section 1.5.4).

All computations ran single-threaded under `nice -n 19`; total CPU well under 10 minutes.

### P17 at a spherical split place

[Proposition 7.3f](../chapters/07-chains-of-theta-lifts.md#7431-spherical-transfer-at-a-split-place) identifies an irreducible local partner via the full-rank germ of a Schwartz pairing. It then computes the spherical Hecke character by lattice counts. R3 applies to the listed split-GL caller and direct rank/germ proof bodies. The deeper mixed-product inputs, vacuum-span proof and the tensor-matching interface retain their former levels. The proposition assumes irreducibility of the candidate induction and a nonzero local pairing.

The source map in §7.6.1 identifies local R3 for unit evaluation and the direct row/column and placement transports. Filtration assembly and Jacquet exactness are cited. ### Chapter 14 absolute Cauchy products

[Chapter section](../chapters/14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#absolute-cauchy-products-in-a-banach-algebra) · [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-14-absolute-cauchy-products). Local R3 covers `tsum_mul_tsum_eq_tsum_sum_antidiagonal_of_summable_norm` through the finite antidiagonal fibers and `FormalMultilinearSeries.summable_norm_apply` through a strictly larger admissible radius and the multilinear norm bound. `exp_add_of_commute_of_mem_ball` consumes both and then applies the commuting binomial identity. The scalar-line derivative uses commuting translation. The general Fréchet derivative uses multilinear recentering. In `spectrum.exp_mem_exp` the scalar shift uses commuting addition and the shifted factorial sum separately uses each continuous multiplication map. `IsSelfAdjoint.mem_spectrum_eq_re` consumes the forward inclusion, and A2 `LConv.lean:154` consumes spectral reality on a star square. The bilateral tail estimates and operator examples are textbook exposition.

The Cauchy section has a same-source preliminary acceptance. The radius, differentiation and spectral sections carry their own verdicts. The two radius corrections qualify the radius-zero converse by `NormOneClass` and pass the adjoint through the sum termwise. Lean kernel replay and A2 Main reachability are separate outstanding checks.

The formal order is Banach exponential and unitary spectrum → ambient real spectrum → connected self-adjoint resolvent and closed-star spectral permanence → complex normal calculus → real non-unital calculus. Real spectral nonnegativity and the real square give the operator star order, which joins the real calculus in supplying nonnegative-real calculus and then `CFC.sqrt`. The [arbitrary-operator trace consumer](../chapters/14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#positive-root-consumer-traceclass) and [compact root consumer](../chapters/14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#positive-root-consumer-compact-spectrum) use that root. Compact decomposition independently uses positivity and compactness. The Hilbert-space proof of the real calculus also has its own operator resolvent estimate and polynomial extension. Lean kernel replay and reachability from Main are pending for this package. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-14-self-adjoint-spectral-reality)

The pointwise integral identity in §14.4.2.7 uses two Bochner-integrable inputs. The extension to the full Hilbert space is a class identity, expressed a.e. in frequency. In §14.4.2.8 the finite-measure exchange argument gives a.e. output agreement. Cauchy–Schwarz gives a pointwise integrable section everywhere for a bounded compact kernel. Keep the A2 `Ker` hypothesis when citing `convSpec` and `fourConv`. The general integrable kernel extension and section strengthening are paper results already proved in the source manuscripts.
